A stacky approach to prismatic crystals via q q -prism charts
Zeyu Liu

Abstract
. Let Y Y be a locally complete intersection over 𝒪 K \mathcal{O}_{K} , the ring of integers of a p p -adic field containing a p p -power root of unity ζ p \zeta_{p} . We classify 𝒟 ⁡ ( Y Δ , 𝒪 Δ ) \mathcal{D}(Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}) by studying quasi-coherent complexes on Y Δ Y^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}} (the prismatization of Y Y ) via q q -prism charts. We also develop a Galois descent mechanism to remove the assumption on 𝒪 K \mathcal{O}_{K} . As an application, we classify 𝒟 ⁡ ( WCart ) \mathcal{D}(\WCart) and give a purely algebraic calculation of the cohomology of the structure sheaf on ( Z p ) Δ (\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}} . Along the way, for Y Y a locally complete intersection over A ¯ \overline{A} with A A lying over a q q -prism, we classify 𝒟 ⁡ ( Y / A Δ , 𝒪 Δ ) \mathcal{D}(Y_{/A}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}) , i.e. quasi-coherent complexes on the relative prismatization of Y Y .



1.
Introduction

In this paper, we work with a
p
-adic field
K
. More precisely, let
\mathcal{O}_{K}
be a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic with fraction field
K
and perfect residue field
k
of characteristic
p
.

Introduced by Bhatt and Scholze in
[
BS22
]
, prismatic cohomology theory turns out to be a ground breaking work in
p
-adic geometry and
p
-adic Hodge theory. It connects various known
p
-adic cohomology theories via specialization, and when passing to coefficients, various prismatic crystals are closely related to
p
-adic Galois representations and
p
-adic local systems. For example, in
[
BS21
]
Bhatt-Scholze have identified
\mathrm{Vect}^{\varphi}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
with the category of crystalline
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems on the generic fiber of
X
for
X=\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, giving new methods of studying
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-crystalline representations of
G_{K}
, a key object in the area of
p
-adic Hodge theory. By working with analytic prismatic
F
-crystals instead, such a result was later generalized to
X
a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
due to the work of Guo-Reinecke
[
GR24
]
or that of Du-Liu-Moon-Shimizu
[
DLMS24
]
. Hence it becomes both important and urgent to understand the underlying prismatic crystals, viewed as objects in
\mathcal{D}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
.

Later prismatic cohomology theory and its variants have been packaged into coherent information on certain stacks, furnished by the pioneering work of Drinfeld
[
Dri20
]
and Bhatt-Lurie
[
BL22a
,
BL22b
]
,
[
Bha22
]
. More precisely, to any (quasi-syntomic)
p
-adic formal scheme
X
, they associate
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, the prismatization of
X
, whose quasi-coherent complexes parameterize prismatic crystals. Also, they introduce
X^{\mathcal{N}}
and
X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
, which admit canonical morphisms
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\to X^{\mathcal{N}}\to X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and further encode information on Nygaard filtrations and Frobenius structures. Under such a stacky language, Bhatt and Lurie have proved that the category of reflexive
F
-gauges on
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\mathrm{Syn}}
is equivalent to the category of lattices in crystalline
representations of
G_{K}
. Also, recently in
[
TVX24
]
Terentiuk-Vologodsky-Xu have established an equivalence between a subcategory of quasi-coherent complexes on
W(k)^{\mathrm{Syn}}
and certain derived category of Fontaine-Laffaille modules. For the purpose of understanding such
F
-gauges, we need to know more about their underlying complexes, obtained by pulling back along
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\to X^{\mathrm{Syn}}
. It thus becomes a fundamental question in
p
-adic Hodge theory to understand
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
.

Towards this direction, Bhatt and Lurie
[
BL22a
,
BL22b
]
have done a detailed study on the structure of the Hodge-Tate stack of
X
, which is a closed substack inside
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
and is denoted as
X^{\operatorname{HT}}
. Their work identifies
X^{\operatorname{HT}}
with the classifying stack of certain groupoids for
X
a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
. Such a geometric description directly leads to the classification of (derived) Hodge-Tate crystals on the prismatic site of
X
, recorded in
[
BL22a
,
BL22b
]
and
[
AHB22
,
AHLB23
]
.

On the other hand, while the geometry of the Hodge-Tate locus
X^{\operatorname{HT}}
is usually well understood, little is known about the structure of
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, even in the simplest case
X=\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
, leaving the description of
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
still mysterious
1
1

1

Indeed, the only known result so far is Gros-Le Stum-Quirós’s
[
GSQ23
]
classification for
\mathrm{Vect}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
when
X=W(k)[\zeta_{p}]
.

. In
[
Liu24
]
we made partial progress on this question. More precisely, we constructed certain nilpotent thickenings of
X^{\operatorname{HT}}=X_{1}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
inside
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}=X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{\infty}
, denoted as
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
in
loc. cit.
, such that
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\simeq\mathcal{D}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{n})
. For
X=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k))
and
n\leq p
, by pulling back to the Breuil-Kisin chart along
\rho:\mathfrak{S}\to\mathrm{Spf}(W(k))^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, we then classify
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
using linear algebraic data (roughly it means that
\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}
is equipped with a monodromy operator
\theta_{\mathcal{E}}
for
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
), see
[
Liu24
, Theorem 1.1]
for details. For a general
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
n
, we further observe that a monodromy operator exists on
\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}
only when restricted to a slight shrinking of
\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n}
, namely the locus obtained by adding
\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}{p}
. Moreover, the proof in
[
Liu24
]
actually implies that this is
optimal
if working with the Breuil-Kisin prism. As a consequence, we classify
\mathcal{D}((\mathcal{O}_{K})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}[[\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}{p}]]/(\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/p)^{n})
for all
n
(hence also
\mathcal{D}((\mathcal{O}_{K})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}[[\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}{p}]])
) in
[
Liu24
, Theorem 1.6]
, which could be viewed as integral models for (truncated)-de Rham prismatic crystals over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
studied in
[
Liu23
]
.

After realizing the mystery of studying
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
via Breuil-Kisin prism charts, it is natural to ask whether the strategy developed in
[
Liu24
]
can be applied to some other charts to get a complete understanding of
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, for
X
a general quasi-syntomic
p
-adic scheme. Motivated by the recent work of Michel Gros, Bernard Le Stum and Adolfo Quirós
[
GSQ23
]
, which classifies
\operatorname{Vect}((W(k)[\zeta_{p}])_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
(hence also
\operatorname{Vect}((W(k)[\zeta_{p}])_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/{\mathcal{I}}^{m})
for all
m
) via absolute
q
-calclus, we turn our attention to the
q
-prism charts in this paper. Our first result is the classification of
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
for
\mathcal{O}_{K}=W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}]
(
\alpha\geq 0
), the cyclotomic ring obtained by adjoining
p^{\alpha+1}
-th root of unity to
W(k)
. To state it, we fix some notations first.

Notation 1.0.1
.

Let
X=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}])
and
(A,d)=(W(k)[[q-1]],[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. Let
\gamma_{A}:A\to A
be the
W(k)
-linear ring automorphism sending
q
to
q^{p^{\alpha+1}+1}
, then
\partial_{A}:=\frac{\gamma_{A}-\mathrm{Id}}{q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)}
is a
\gamma_{A}
-derivation of
A
, i.e.
\partial_{A}(x_{1}x_{2})=\gamma_{A}(x_{1})\partial_{A}(x_{2})+\partial_{A}(x_{1})x_{2}
. We define the
Ore extension

A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
to be the noncommutative ring obtained by giving the ring of polynomials
A[\partial]
a new multiplication law, subject to the identity

\partial r=\gamma_{A}(r)\partial+\partial_{A}(r),~~~\forall r\in A.

Theorem 1.0.2

(
Theorem

3.3.4
. Quasi-coherent complexes on the prismatization of cyclotomic rings)
.

Assume that
p>2
or
\alpha>0
. With the preceding notations, for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the pullback along the covering
\rho:\mathrm{Spf}(A/d^{n})\to X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
2
2

2

By abuse of notation, when
n=\infty
, this just means
\rho:\mathrm{Spf}(A)\to X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
.
induces a fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\beta_{n}^{+}:\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(A/d^{n}[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]),\qquad\mathcal{E}\mapsto(\rho^{*}(\mathcal{E}),\partial_{\mathcal{E}}),

whose essential image consists of those objects
M\in\mathcal{D}(A/d^{n}[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}])
satisfying the following pair of conditions:

•

M
is
(p,d)
-complete.

•

The action of
\partial
on the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{*}(M\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}k)

3
3

3

Here the derived tensor product means the derived base change along
A/d^{n}\to A/(d,q-1)=k
.
is locally nilpotent.

In particular, per
Remark

3.1.16
, for
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
,

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{fib}(\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{\mathcal{E}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}).

Remark 1.0.3
.

•

Theorem

1.0.2
fails for
p=2
and
\alpha=0
. Indeed, the assumption that
p>2
or
\alpha>0
precisely separates out the ramified case and Bhatt-Lurie’s work
[
BL22a
,
BL22b
]
shows that
\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{\operatorname{HT}}
for
\mathcal{O}_{K}
ramified behaves quite differently from
\mathrm{Spf}(W(k))^{\operatorname{HT}}
. It is still
false
when
p=2,\alpha=0
even if we modify the nilpotence condition in the statement, namely instead requiring
\partial^{2}-\partial
being locally nilpotent. Actually, the failure already shows up on the Hodge-Tate locus for cohomology reasons, see
Remark

3.2.5
for details.

•

One can restrict
Theorem

1.0.2
to perfect complexes (resp. vector bundles) to get the corresponding classification for
\mathrm{Perf}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
(resp.
\mathrm{Vect}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
), see
Corollary

3.3.5
for details.

•

Our results are new for
\alpha>0
. When
\alpha=0
and restricted to the abelian level (i.e. working with vector bundles),
[
GSQ23
]
identifies
\operatorname{Vect}((W(k)[\zeta_{p}])_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
with the category of finite projective
A
modules equipped with a
q(p)
-connection, denoted as
\nabla_{q(p)}(A)
in
loc.cit.
. Given
(M,\partial_{M})\in\nabla_{q(p)}(A)
, one can check that the Leibnitz rule on
\partial_{M}
given in
[
GSQ23
]
precisely promotes such a pair to an
A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
-module, whose underlying module is just
M
and
\partial
acts on it via
\partial_{M}
. In this sense, our
Theorem

1.0.2
is a generalization of the main theorem in
[
GSQ23
]
to derived coefficients when
\alpha=0
. However, our methods are different. While they use the prismatic site, we adopt the perspective furnished by Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie’s stacks as well as the strategy working beyond the Hodge-Tate locus developed in
[
Liu24
]
.

•

When
n=1
, note that
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X^{\operatorname{HT}})
is equipped with two operators: the
q
-connection
\partial_{\mathcal{E}}
from
Theorem

1.0.2
(constructed via the
q
prism) as well as the Sen operator
\theta_{\mathcal{E}}
from
[
AHB22
, Theorem 2.5]
(constructed utilizing the Breuil-Kisin prism), hence so is
\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}
. The relations between the action of
\partial_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\theta_{\mathcal{E}}
on

\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}
can be summarized as follows:

\partial_{\mathcal{E}}=d^{\prime}(q)\cdot\frac{(1+p^{\alpha+1})^{\theta_{\mathcal{E}}/E^{\prime}(\pi)}-1}{p^{\alpha+1}}.

see
Remark

3.4.5
for details.

•

In
p
-adic Hodge theory, the existence of the Frobenius structure typically forces the monodromy operator to be nilpotent. Taking
Theorem

1.0.2
as an input, arguing similarly as that in
[
GSQ23
, Corollary 10.9, Corollary 10.11]
(which treats the case
\alpha=0
), one can show that the category of
q
-connections in vector bundles over
A
equipped with an additional Frobenius structure is equivalent to the category of prismatic
F
-crystals in vector bundles on
(\mathcal{O}_{K})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
for
\mathcal{O}_{K}=(W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}]
, hence also parameterize crystalline
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-representations of
G_{K}
by invoking
[
BS21
, Theorem 5.6]
.

We now explain the idea of proving
Theorem

1.0.2
in more detail, as this strategy works uniformly to prove several upcoming theorems. For a moment we assume
X
is a smooth affine
p
-adic formal scheme and
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

As pointed out at the very beginning, it’s typically difficult to describe the structure of
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
unless
n=1
(For
X=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k))
, we do know the structure of
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n}
for
n\leq p
due to a result of Sasha Petrov, see
[
Liu24
, Proposition 3.17]
). This roughly means that it is hard to understand
\square
, the fiber product of the following diagram

\textstyle{\square\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(B/J^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(B/J^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n}}

for any transversal prism
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
covering the final object in the topos and
n>1
, here
\eta_{n}
is the induced covering map. When translating this picture into the prismatic site, this means
B^{(1)}/J^{n}
is too complicated to understand for
n>1

4
4

4

When
n=1
, it is always a divided power polynomial algebra over
B/J
due to the work of Tian
[
Tia23
]
.
, where
B^{(1)}
is the self-product of
(B,J)
in
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. All of the known work for classifying various prismatic crystals via the prismatic site relies on an explicit calculation of
B^{(1)}
(and its variants) for suitable
B
, from which they deduce a Sen/monodromy operator on the evaluation of prismatic crystals at
(B,J)
by identifying prismatic crystals with stratification over the simplicial diagram
B^{(\bullet)}
, which (plus certain nilpotence condition on this operator) is essentially enough for reconstructing the corresponding prismatic crystals. To name a few,
[
MT20
]
,
[
Tia23
]
,
[
GMW23a
]
,
[
MW22
]
,
[
Ogu22
]
,
[
Liu23
]
,
[
GMW23b
]
,
[
GSQ23
]
,
[
Tsu24
]
,
[
GMW24
]
.

A key observation in
[
Liu24
]
, which is inspired by the recent work of Bhatt-Lurie
[
BL22a
]
as well as that of Anschütz-Heuer-Le Bras
[
AHB22
]
for
n=1
, is that to produce a monodromy operator on
\eta^{*}\mathcal{E}
for
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, it actually doesn’t require the full understanding of
\square
. Instead, it suffices to work with an infinitesimal neighborhood of the diagonal embedding
\mathrm{Spf}(B/J^{n})\xhookrightarrow{}\square
. More precisely, as long as we can construct certain nontrivial ring homomorphism
\psi=\mathrm{Id}+\epsilon\nabla:B/J^{n}\to B/J^{n}\oplus B/J^{n}\cdot\epsilon
5
5

5

The algebraic structure on the target is to be determined later. Indeed,
\epsilon^{2}
is not always
0
unless we work with the Breuil-Kisin prism.
and specify an isomorphism
\gamma
between two functors
\eta\circ\psi
and
\eta\circ\iota
6
6

6

Here
\iota:B/J^{n}\to B/J^{n}\oplus B/J^{n}\cdot\epsilon
is the canonical inclusion.
, i.e. the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(B/J^{n}\oplus B/J^{n}\cdot\epsilon)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(B/J^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(B/J^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},}

then for any
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
,
\gamma
will induce an isomorphism

\gamma:\psi^{*}\eta^{*}(\mathscr{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\iota^{*}\eta^{*}\mathscr{E},

which essentially leads to a monodromy operator
\nabla_{\mathcal{E}}:\eta^{*}\mathcal{E}\to\eta^{*}\mathcal{E}
satisfying certain Leibnitz rules which depends on the algebraic structure on
B/J^{n}\oplus B/J^{n}\cdot\epsilon
.

From such a uniform stacky point of view,

•

In
[
Liu24
]
, we considered the case
X=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k))
and constructed such a diagram for the Breuil-Kisin prism
(B,J)=(W(k)[[\lambda]],(\lambda))
when
n\leq p
(this bound is optimal, see
[
Liu24
, Remark 3.2]
). Also, again using the Breuil-Kisin prism, for a general
X=\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
and
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, we constructed such a diagram for
\widetilde{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{[n]}}
(see
[
Liu24
, Definition 4.3]
). Such constructions essentially lead to the classification results
[
Liu24
, Theorem 1.1, Theorem 1.6]
stated at the beginning of the introduction.

•

Let
X=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}])
and
(B,J)=(W(k)[[q-1]],[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. We construct the above diagram for
\psi:B\to B[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}-q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\epsilon)
, from which we obtain the
q
-connections in
Theorem

1.0.2
. The complexity of the
q
-twisted Leibnitz rule comes from the complicated algebraic structure on the target of
\psi
, which is a new phenomenon when working with the
q
-prism.

•

When
X=\mathrm{Spf}(R)
is small affine over
\overline{A}
(in the sense of Construction
1.0.13
) with
(A,d)
lying over a
q
-prism, we construct the above diagram for
\psi:\tilde{R}\to\tilde{R}\oplus\epsilon\Omega_{\tilde{R}/A}
with
(\epsilon dT_{i})^{2}=(q-1)T_{i}\cdot\epsilon dT_{i}
on the target, from which we extract the
q
-Higgs derivations in
Theorem

1.0.14
.

Remark 1.0.4
.

Comparing the arithmetic case and the geometric case above, we see
q
plays the role of a coordinate
T_{i}
.

Remark 1.0.5
.

Beyond the benefit of dealing with derived coefficients uniformly, there are several other advantages of developing such a stacky method.

•

First, it’s usually much easier to observe certain integral structures from the stacky perspective. For example, in
[
Liu24
]
we were able to classify
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
for
n\leq p
as well as
\mathcal{D}((\mathcal{O}_{K})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}[[\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}{p}]]/(\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/p)^{n})
for all
n
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, while working with prismatic cite in
[
Liu23
]
and
[
GMW23b
]
only deals with the isogeny category
\mathrm{Vect}((\mathcal{O}_{K})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{n}[\frac{1}{p}])
(i.e. with
p
-inverted) for
n\geq 2
and it’s unclear to see how to refine arguments there to integral levels.

•

Moreover, the stacky approach allows us to work beyond the smooth case without much extra effort, cf.
Theorem

1.0.14
,
Theorem

1.0.17
, which is even necessary to understand
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
for
X=\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
a point, see the discussion after
Remark

1.0.12
for details. On the other hand, in every work studying prismatic crystals via the prismatic site we mentioned, a local description is given only over a framed smooth lifting except Ogus’
[
Ogu22
]
and Tsuji’s
[
Tsu24
]
.

•

Finally, in comparison with the classical methods working with the prismatic site and hence requiring the calculation of
B^{(1)}
, we only need to construct the above diagram, which is usually much simpler, see the proof of
Proposition

3.1.9
for a typical example. Moreover, a key step when working with the prismatic site is to reconstruct a prismatic crystal starting with a monodromy operator, which amounts to checking certain cocycle conditions that always involve heavy calculations. On the other hand, following a strategy of Bhatt-Lurie, the essential image of
\beta_{n}^{+}
in
Theorem

1.0.2
can be determined easily once we prove full faithfulness and know the generators of the source.

On the other hand, the
q
-prism chart was used slightly differently by Bhatt-Lurie
[
BL22a
]
when studying quasi-coherent complexes on the Cartier-Witt stack, namely they realize the latter as a quotient of the
q
-prism. At the very end of the preparation of this project, we realize that we can play a similar game for
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
when
X=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}])
and give an alternative classification of
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, more akin to Bhatt-Lurie’s approach in
[
BL22a
, Section 3.8]
(we will explain the relation of this description with
Theorem

1.0.2
in
Remark

1.0.7
). Indeed, for
(A,I)=(W(k)[[q-1]],[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, it admits an action by
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, where an element
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
acts by the automorphism
q\mapsto q^{u}
, which will be denoted as
\gamma_{u}
. In
Proposition

3.4.1
, we show that the cover map
\rho:\mathrm{Spf}(A)\to X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
factors through the quotient stack
[\mathrm{Spf}(A)/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}]
and moreover, it sits in the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{[\mathrm{Spf}(A/(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1))/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q^{p^{\alpha}}\mapsto 1}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{Spf}(A)/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A/(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}.}

Then an alternative description of
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
is given as follows:

Theorem 1.0.6

(
Theorem

3.4.7
)
.

Let

. Assume that

or

. Then the diagram above induces a fully faithful functor of

-categories

F:\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(A)/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}])\times_{\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(A/(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1))/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}])}\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(A/(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1))).

The essential image of

consists of those pairs
(M,\gamma_{M})
with

and
\gamma_{M}
being an automorphism of
M
which is congruent to the identity modulo

such that the following holds:

•

M\in\mathcal{D}(A)
is
(p,d)
-complete.

•

the action of
\partial_{M}
on the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{*}(M\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}k)

7
7

7

Here the derived tensor product means the derived base change along

.
is locally nilpotent, where
\partial_{M}:M\to M
is an operator satisfying

.

In particular, for
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, the diagram

(1.1)

is a pullback square in the

\hat{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Remark 1.0.7

(The relation between
Theorem

1.0.6
with
Theorem

1.0.2
)
.

Let
u=1+p^{\alpha+1}
, which is a topological generator of
(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
, then
\gamma_{u}=\gamma_{A}
and for
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, our construction of
\partial_{\mathcal{E}}
implies that
\gamma_{u}
acts on
\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}
via
1+q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\partial_{\mathcal{E}}
(see
Proposition

3.4.3
). Consequently, the pullback from
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
to
\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(A)/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}])
along

\rho:[\mathrm{Spf}(A)/(1+p^{\alpha+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}]\to X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
is not fully faithful yet (see
Remark

3.4.4
for details), and to eliminate such a difference, we need to utilize the above diagram. However, it is
Theorem

1.0.2
which can be more easily generalized to the high dimensional case, as the forthcoming
Theorem

1.0.14
and
Theorem

1.0.17
justifies.

Before moving on to work with a general
X
, we discuss how to apply
Theorem

1.0.2
or
Theorem

1.0.6
to understand
\mathcal{D}(\WCart)
via Galois descent theory, as suggested by
[
BL22a
]
. For this purpose, we tentatively assume
X=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\zeta_{p}]
and consider the
q
-prism
(A,d)=(\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[q-1]],[p]_{q})\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. As discussed in
[
BL22a
, Section 3.5]

A=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[q-1]]
admits an action of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
, which carries each element
e\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
to the automorphism of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[[q-1]]
given by
\gamma_{e}:q\mapsto q^{[e]}
, for which
[e]
is the Teichmuller lift of
e
. One can see that such an action preserves the ideal
(d)
, hence induces an automorphism
\gamma_{e}:A/d^{n}\to A/d^{n}
for any
n\geq 1
. As an upshot,
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
acts on
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. Moreover, the natural morphism
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\to\WCart
is
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
-equivariant, with the target given the trivial action. Hence it induces a natural morphism
\pi:[X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n}/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}]\to\WCart_{n}
(see
Proposition

3.5.1
for details). Then our main result is the following:

Theorem 1.0.8

(
Theorem

3.5.15
)
.

Assume
p>2
. Then pullback along
\pi
induces a functor

F:\mathcal{D}(\WCart)\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}([X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}])\times_{[\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}]}\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})),

which turns out to be fully faithful. Moreover, the essential image of
F
consists of those
\mathcal{E}
in the target whose underlying complex in
\mathcal{D}([X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}])
(still denoted as
\mathcal{E}
by abuse of notation) satisfies the following additional condition:

•

the
q
-Higgs connection
\partial_{\mathcal{E}_{0}}
of
\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\mathcal{E}|_{X^{\operatorname{HT}}}
can be factored as
e\circ\partial_{\mathcal{E}_{0}}^{\prime}
such that the action of
(\partial_{\mathcal{E}_{0}}^{\prime})^{p}-\partial_{\mathcal{E}_{0}}^{\prime}
on the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}k)
is locally nilpotent, here
e=d^{\prime}(q)\in W(k)[\zeta_{p}]
.

Combining
Theorem

1.0.2
with
Theorem

1.0.8
, we can understand
\mathcal{D}(\WCart)
quite well.

Corollary 1.0.9

(
Corollary

3.5.17
)
.

We have a fully faithful functor

F:\mathcal{D}(\WCart)\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}])/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}])\times_{[\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}]}\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})).

Moreover, the essential image of
F
consists of those
\mathcal{E}
in the target whose underlying complex in
\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}])/\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}])
(still denoted as
\mathcal{E}
by abuse of notation) satisfies the following additional condition:

•

the action of
\partial
on
\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\mathcal{E}|_{X^{\operatorname{HT}}}
can be factored as
e\circ\partial^{\prime}
such that the action of
(\partial^{\prime})^{p}-\partial^{\prime}
on the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{*}(\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}k)
is locally nilpotent, here
e=d^{\prime}(q)\in W(k)[\zeta_{p}]
.

Remark 1.0.10
.

One can compare our
Theorem

1.0.8
and
Corollary

1.0.9

with
[
BL22a
, Theorem 3.8.3]
, in which they prove that there is a fully faithful functor

F^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}(\WCart)\to\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(A)/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}])\times_{\mathcal{D}([\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}])}\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})),

which is not essentially surjective (see
[
BL22a
, Warning 3.7.12]
). More or less, given
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(\WCart)
corresponding to
(M,\partial_{M})
under
Corollary

1.0.9
, the information of
\partial_{M}
is hidden in the
(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}
action on
F^{\prime}(\mathcal{E})
as
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}=(1+p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\times}\rtimes\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\times}
, see
[
BL22a
, Propostition 3.7.1]
. In this sense,
[
BL22a
, Theorem 3.8.3]
could be viewed as a combination of
Theorem

1.0.6
and
Theorem

1.0.8
, while
Corollary

1.0.9
combines
Theorem

1.0.2
and
Theorem

1.0.8
instead.

Another application of
Theorem

1.0.8
is the calculation of the cohomology of the structure sheaf on
(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
.

Proposition 1.0.11

(
Proposition

3.5.14
)
.

Assume that
p>2
. The Cartier-Witt stack
\WCart
is of cohomological dimension
1
. Moreover,

\mathrm{H}^{0}(\WCart,\mathcal{O})=\mathbb{Z}_{p},~~~\quad\mathrm{H}^{1}(\WCart,\mathcal{O})\simeq\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N},n\not\equiv p\mod p+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Remark 1.0.12
.

The cohomology of the structure sheaf on
\WCart
was known before, for example,
\mathrm{H}^{0}(\WCart,\mathcal{O})=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is stated in
[
Dri20
, Corollary 4.7.2]
. Via topological methods, in
[
BMS19
]
Bhatt-Morrow-Scholze show that
\mathrm{H}^{1}((\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})=\prod_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}.
Note that there is a bijection between the sets
\{n\in\mathbb{N}|n\not\equiv p\mod p+1\}
and
\mathbb{N}
.

Next we proceed to discuss the classification of
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
for a general
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and we can separate this question into three cases.

•

If
\mathcal{O}_{K}=W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}]
is a cyclotomic ring, then this was already solved by
Theorem

1.0.2
.

•

If
\mathcal{O}_{K}
doesn’t contain
\zeta_{p}
, then we can consider
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{K}[\zeta_{p}]
first, and then run Galois descent similar as that in
Theorem

1.0.8
provided we can understand
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\prime})^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
.

•

If
\mathcal{O}_{K}
contains
\zeta_{p}
, then
\mathcal{O}_{K}
could be viewed as a locally complete intersection over
\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}=W(k)[\zeta_{p}]
. Indeed, if we choose a uniformizer
\pi\in\mathcal{O}_{K}
and suppose
E(u)
is the minimal polynomial of
\pi
over
W(k)[\frac{1}{p}]
, suppose
E(u)
factored as
\prod_{i=0}^{t}E_{i}
over
\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}
with
E_{i}
monic and irreducible, WLOG we may assume
\pi
is a root of
E_{0}
(then
E_{i}(\pi)\neq 0
for
i>0
as
E
is irreducible), then
\mathcal{O}_{K}=\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}[u]/E_{0}(u)
.

With the discussion above, the question turns into classifying
\mathcal{D}(Y^{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}})
for
Y
a locally complete intersection over
\mathcal{O}_{K}=W(k)[\zeta_{p}]
. Fix
(A,d)=(W(k)[[q-1]],[p]_{q})\in(W(k)[\zeta_{p}])_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
as usual. By considering the following pullback square,

\textstyle{Y_{/A}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{A}}
\textstyle{Y^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},}

where
Y_{/A}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
is the relative prismatization, we could proceed in two steps. First, we need to understand how
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
differs from
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Spf}(A))
, which was exactly illustrated in
Theorem

1.0.2
. Secondly, we would like to have a characterization of
\mathcal{D}(Y_{/A}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
. It turns out for the purpose of classifying complexes on the relative prismatization, we can work under a much more general setting: we don’t need to assume the base prism is exactly the
q
-prism
(W(k)[[q-1]],[p]_{q})
, instead, any prism lying over it works. More precisely,

Construction 1.0.13
.

Fix
(A,d)
to be a transversal prism over

. Let

be a small affine over

with a fixed chart

. Moreover, this étale chart map uniquely lifts to a prism
(\tilde{R},I)
over
(A\left\langle\underline{T}\right\rangle,[p]_{q})

(

) be a closed embedding such that the prismatic envelope with respect to the morphism of

\tilde{R}\{\frac{x_{1},\cdots,x_{r}}{d}\}^{\wedge}_{\delta}
8
8

8

It is obtained by freely adjoining

to

in the category of derived

-complete simplicial

A

[Corollary 2.44] for the precise definition. It is a derived ring that might not be concentrated on degree

.
, denoted as
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)
later. One can show that (
Proposition

4.2.4
) such data induces a covering map

Theorem 1.0.14

(
Theorem

4.1.23
,
Theorem

4.2.11

.

Let

. For

. Then the covering map

above induces a fully faithful functor
9
9

9

By abuse of notation, when

to denote
\tilde{R}
.

\displaystyle\beta_{n}^{+}:\mathcal{D}(Y_{/A,n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)/d^{n}[\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)},\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}]),\qquad\mathcal{E}\mapsto(\rho^{*}(\mathcal{E}),\nabla_{\mathcal{E}}),

The non-commutative ring
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)/d^{n}[\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)},\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}]
appearing above is defined as follows:

Definition 1.0.15
.

We define the
Ore extension

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)},\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}]
to be the noncommutative ring obtained by giving the ring of polynomials
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\nabla_{1},\cdots,\nabla_{m}]
a new multiplication law, subject to the identity

\begin{split}\nabla_{i}\cdot\nabla_{j}&=\nabla_{j}\cdot\nabla_{i},~~~\forall~1\leq i,j\leq m\\
\nabla_{i}\cdot r&=\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}(r)\cdot\nabla_{i}+\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}(r),~~~\forall~r\in{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),1\leq i\leq m.\end{split}

Here
\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}
(resp.
\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}
) is the ring automorphism (resp.
\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}
-derivation) on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)
defined in
Remark

4.2.6
, extending
\gamma_{\tilde{R},i}
(resp.
\nabla_{\tilde{R},i}
) on

defined above

4.1.1
.

Remark 1.0.16
.

Recently Tsuji studied
\mathrm{Vect}((Y/A)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
via relative prismatic site in
[
Tsu24
]
. He proved that when
X
is small affine over the
m
-dimensional torus over
A
, there is a canonical equivalence between
\mathrm{Vect}((Y/A)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
and the category of
D
-modules with quasi-nilpotent

-Higgs field. Moreover, he was also able to calculate the cohomology of a prismatic crystal in terms of the cohomology of the associated
q
-Higgs complex, see
[
Tsu24
, Theorem 0.1, Theorem 0.2]
. Such results should be closely related to
Theorem

1.0.14
when restricted to the abelian level. But again, our methods are totally different as we follow the stacky roadmap directed by Drinfeld, Bhatt-Lurie and apply the strategy working beyond the Hodge-Tate locus developed in
[
Liu24
]
.

Following the picture illustrated before Construction
1.0.13
, essentially by combing the tool-kits proving

1.0.2
and

for

a locally complete intersection over

Theorem 1.0.17

(

,

)

. We further assume

with

or

. Then for

, the covering map

induces a fully faithful functor
10
10

10

Similarly as before, when
Y=X
, we use
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(X)
to denote
\tilde{R}
.

\displaystyle\beta_{n}^{+}:\mathcal{D}(Y_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)/d^{n}[\partial,\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{\tilde{R}}]),\qquad\mathcal{E}\mapsto(\rho_{Y}^{*}(\mathcal{E}),\partial_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla_{\mathcal{E}}),

whose essential image consists of those objects
M\in\mathcal{D}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)/d^{n}[\partial,\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{\tilde{R}}])
satisfying the following pair of conditions:

•

is

-adically complete.

•

The action of
\partial
and
\nabla_{i}
on the cohomology
\mathrm{H}^{*}(M\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)/d^{n}}^{\mathbb{L}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)/(d,q-1))

is locally nilpotent for all
i
.

In particular, by
Corollary

5.1.8
, for
\mathcal{E}\in\mathcal{D}(Y_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
,

\mathrm{R}\Gamma(Y_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\operatorname{fib}(\mathrm{DR}(\rho_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{E},\nabla_{\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{\tilde{\partial}_{\mathcal{E}}^{[\bullet]}}\mathrm{DR}(\rho_{Y}^{*}\mathcal{E},\nabla_{\mathcal{E}})).

The non-commutative ring
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\partial,\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}]
just adds one variable
\partial
to
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)},\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}]
to encode the arithmetic information, and the relations with other variables are given below.

Definition 1.0.18

(
Definition

5.1.6
,
Remark

5.2.2
)
.

We define the
Ore extension

{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\partial,\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}]
to be the noncommutative ring obtained by giving the ring of polynomials
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\partial,\nabla_{1},\cdots,\nabla_{m}]
a new multiplication law, subject to the identity

\begin{split}\nabla_{i}\cdot\nabla_{j}&=\nabla_{j}\cdot\nabla_{i},~~~\forall~1\leq i,j\leq m,\\
\nabla_{i}\cdot r&=\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}(r)\cdot\nabla_{i}+\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}(r),~~~\forall~r\in\tilde{R},1\leq i\leq m,\\
\partial\cdot r&=\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),0}(r)\cdot\partial+\partial_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)}(r),~~~\forall~r\in\tilde{R},\\
\partial\cdot\nabla_{i}&=s_{0}^{-1}(1+\beta q\mathscr{D}(\nabla_{i}))\nabla_{i}\cdot\partial+(s_{0}^{-1}\mathscr{D}(\nabla_{i})-s_{1})\cdot\nabla_{i},~~~\forall~1\leq i\leq m.\end{split}

Here
\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}
(resp.
\partial
for
i=0
and
\nabla_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}
for
i\neq 0
) is the ring automorphism (resp.
\gamma_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y),i}
-derivation) on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)
defined in
Remark

4.2.6
, extending
\gamma_{\tilde{R},i}
(resp.
\partial_{\tilde{R}}
,
\nabla_{\tilde{R},i}
) on
\tilde{R}
defined above
Lemma

5.1.1
.
s_{0},s_{1},\mathscr{D}(\nabla_{i})
are defined in
Lemma

5.1.1
.

Remark 1.0.19
.

•

To the best knowledge of the author,
Theorem

1.0.17
is the most general result so far. In the interesting case that
Y
is a locally complete intersection over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
(for example,
Y
is a small affine over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
) with
\mathcal{O}_{K}
containing
\zeta_{p}
, then following the discussion after
Remark

1.0.12
, we can write
Y
as a locally complete intersection over
\mathcal{O}_{K_{0}}=W(k)[\zeta_{p}]
, hence we could apply
Theorem

1.0.17
to describe
\mathcal{D}(Y_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
, i.e.
\mathcal{D}(Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{n})
for
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. Even in the case that
\mathcal{O}_{K}
doesn’t contain
\zeta_{p}
, we could run Galois descent and reduce to the previous case. An analog of
Theorem

1.0.8
to this full generality will appear in an upcoming work.

•

For
X
a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, recently Gao-Min-Wang

]

\mathrm{Vect}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{n}[\frac{1}{p}])
for any

(and hence
\mathrm{Vect}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},{\mathbb{B}_{\mathrm{dR}}^{+}})
) using prismatic site and Breuil-Kisin prisms. On the other hand, it is known that the results in
[

]
still hold for such a general

. Actually, in an upcoming joint work, for

a locally complete intersection over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, we give a classification of
\mathcal{D}(Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}[[\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}{p}]]/(\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/p)^{n})
for all

(hence also
\mathcal{D}(Y_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}[[\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}{p}]])
) via Breuil-Kisin charts, which are integral models for de Rham prismatic crystals. Moreover, we will relate them with local systems over certain integral period sheaves on the

-site of the generic fiber of

studied in
[
LMN+24
]
, which appears naturally in
loc.cit.
when they study a stacky

-adic Riemann-Hilbert correspondence.

•

Considering the special case
Y=\mathrm{Spf}(W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}])/\pi^{m}

11

It is a non-trivial result to see such a scheme satisfies our assumption on

, see

.
, where

provides a concrete complex to calculate the cohomology of the structure sheaf on

(this is exactly how we prove

•

Heuristically one can think of
\mathcal{D}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{X}(Y)[\partial,\underline{\nabla};\gamma_{\tilde{R}}])
as the derived category of
enhanced
q
-Higgs modules
, where the notation latter is encouraged by the work of Min-Wang

, where they identify Hodge-Tate crystals, i.e.

with the category of enhanced Higgs modules over

.

Outline

The paper is organized as follows. In section 2 we recollect some constructions and properties of the (truncated) prismatization functor. Section 3 explains the construction of the
q
-Higgs connection on
\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E})
for
\mathscr{E}\in\mathcal{D}((W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}])^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
and we prove
Theorem

1.0.2
and
Theorem

1.0.6
. Moreover we develop the Galosi descent machine and prove
Theorem

1.0.8
as well as several related results. In section 4 we study quasi-coherent complexes on the relative prismatization of
Y
over
A
, for
A
a transversal prism over the
q
-prism and prove
Theorem

1.0.14
. Finally in section 5, we study
\mathcal{D}(Y^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
for
Y
a locally complete intersection over a cyclotomic ring and obtain
Theorem

1.0.17
.

Notations and conventions

•

In this paper
\mathcal{O}_{K}
is a complete discrete valuation ring of mixed characteristic with fraction field
K
and perfect residue field
k
of characteristic
p
.

•

For
X
a
p
-adic bounded formal scheme,
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
(resp.
X^{\operatorname{HT}}
) is the prismatization of
X
(resp. the Hodge-Tate stack of
X
) defined as
\WCart_{X}
(resp.
\WCart_{X}^{\operatorname{HT}}
) in
[
BL22a
]
and
[
BL22b
]
. But when
X=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, we stick to the original notion
\WCart
and
\WCart^{\operatorname{HT}}
. Moreover, if
X
is over a base prism
(A,I)
, we write
X_{/A}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
for the relative prismatization of
X
over
A
, denoted as
\WCart_{X/A}
in
[
BL22b
]
.

•

[n]_{q}:=\frac{q^{n}-1}{q-1}=1+q+\cdots+q^{n-1}
.

•

When
R
is a non-commutative ring, we write
\mathcal{D}(R)
for the derived category of left
R
-modules.

2.
Recollections on the prismatization functor

Motivated by Bhatt and Lurie’s definition of
\WCart^{\mathrm{HT}}
(see
[
BL22a
]
), in
[
Liu24
]
we define certain nilpotent thickenings of
\WCart^{\mathrm{HT}}
inside
\WCart
.

Definition 2.0.1

(
[
Liu24
, Definition 2.1]
)
.

Let
R
be a
p
-nilpotent commutative ring. Fix a positive integer
n
, We let
\WCart^{\mathrm{HT}}_{n}(R)
denote the full subcategory of
\WCart(R)

spanned by those Cartier-Witt divisors
\alpha:I\rightarrow W(R)
for which the composite map
I^{\otimes n}\xrightarrow{\alpha^{\otimes n}}W(R)\twoheadrightarrow R

is equal to zero. The construction
R\mapsto\WCart^{\mathrm{HT}}_{n}(R)
determines a closed substack of the Cartier-Witt stack
\WCart
.
We denote this closed substack by
\WCart_{n}
.

In particular, when
n=1
,
\WCart_{1}
coincides with
\WCart^{\mathrm{HT}}
and we will switch freely between these two notations. In general,
\WCart_{n}
could be viewed as a infinitesimal thickening of
\WCart^{\mathrm{HT}}
. To unify the notation, sometimes we write
\WCart_{\infty}
for
\WCart
in this paper.

Remark 2.0.2
.

As pointed out in
[
Liu24
, Remark 2.3]
, there is an alternative definition for
\WCart_{n}
. We briefly review it here. Recall that given a Cartier-Witt divisor
I\rightarrow W(R)
, its base change along the restriction map
W(R)\rightarrow R
is a generalized Cartier divisor. Consequently this determines a morphism of stacks
\mu:\WCart\to[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, which actually factors through the substack
[\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
as the image of
I
in
R
is nilpotent (see
[
BL22a
, Remark 3.1.6]
for details). From this point of view, unwinding
Definition

2.0.1
, we see that the diagram

\textstyle{\WCart_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\WCart\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mu}
\textstyle{[\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}[[t]]/t^{n})/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}

is a pullback square, which gives an equivalent definition of
\WCart_{n}
.

Following
[
BL22b
, Construction 3.7]
, we can generalize the previous construction to any bounded
p
-adic formal scheme
X
.

Construction 2.0.3

(
[
Liu24
, Construction 2.7]
)
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme. Given
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, form a fiber square

\textstyle{X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{WCart}_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{WCart}}

defining the closed substack
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
inside
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, the prismatization of
X
. We will call
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}

the
n
-truncated prismatization of
X
.

With such a definition in hand,
[
BL22b
, Proposition 8.13, Proposition 8.15]
imply that quasi-coherent complexes on
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
corresponds to prismatic crystals on
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
under mild assumption on
X
:

Proposition 2.0.4

(
[
BL22b
, Proposition 8.13, Proposition 8.15]
, see
[
Liu24
, Proposition 2.11]
)
.

Assume that
X
is a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme, then there is an equivalence

\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\lim_{(A,I)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(A/I^{n})=:\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{n})

of symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories. Similar results hold if we consider perfect complexes or vector bundles on both sides instead.

Finally we quickly explain how to pass from a prism in
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
to an affine point of
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
following
[
BL22b
, Construction 3.10]
. Such a construction will be used frequently in this paper without further notice.

Construction 2.0.5
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme. Fix an object
(\operatorname{Spf}(A)\leftarrow\operatorname{Spf}(\bar{A})\rightarrow X)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. Given
(p,I)
-nilpotent
A
-algebra
R
, the structure morphism
A\to R
uniquely lifts to
\delta
-algebra homomorphism
A\rightarrow W(R)
, along which the base change of the inclusion
I\rightarrow A
determines a Cartier-Witt divisor
I\otimes_{A}W(R)\rightarrow W(R)
together with a map
\eta:\mathrm{Spec}(\overline{W(R)})\to\mathrm{Spf}(\bar{A})\to X
of derived schemes. Letting
R
vary, such construction induces a morphism
\rho_{A}:\mathrm{Spf}(A)\rightarrow X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. Moreover,
Then
\rho_{A}
carries the formal subscheme
\mathrm{Spf}(A/I^{n})\subset\mathrm{Spf}(A)
to
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, and therefore restricts to a morphism
\rho_{n,A}:\mathrm{Spf}(A/I^{n})\rightarrow X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
. Later we will omit
n
and
A
when both of them are clear in the context.

Given
\rho_{n,A}
above, one could define the
n
-truncated relative prismatization
.

Construction 2.0.6
.

Let
X
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme. Given
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, form a fiber square

\textstyle{X_{/A,n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A/I^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{A,n}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(\bar{A})_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}

defining the closed substack
X_{/A,n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
inside
X_{/A}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, the relative prismatization of
X
. We will call
X_{/A,n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}

the
n
-truncated relative prismatization of
X
.

Then the following analog of
Proposition

2.0.4
is due to
[
BL22b
, Theorem 6.5]
.

Proposition 2.0.7
.

Let
(A,I)
be a prism and
X
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme over
\bar{A}
, then there is an equivalence

\mathcal{D}_{qc}(X_{/A,n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\lim_{(A,I)\in(X/A)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(A/I^{n})=:\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}((X/A)_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}/\mathcal{I}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}^{n})

of symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories. Similar results hold if we consider perfect complexes or vector bundles on both sides instead.

3.
q
-connections on the prismatization of the absolute cyclotomic ring

In this section we work with
\mathcal{O}_{K}=W(k)[\zeta_{p^{\alpha+1}}]
(
\alpha\geq 0
) and the
q
-prism
(A,I)=(W(k)[[q-1]],[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})
.
X
in this section refers to
\mathrm{Spf}(\mathcal{O}_{K})
. As
(A,I)
defines a transversal prism in
X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, we have a faithfully flat cover
\rho:\mathrm{Spf}(A)\to X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
as explained in Construction
2.0.5
. Our goal is to construct a
q
-connection
\partial
on
\rho^{*}\mathscr{E}
for
\mathscr{E}\in\Qcoh(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
following the strategy explained in the introduction and then study its behaviors. For that purpose, we need several preliminaries.

3.1.
Construction of the
q
-connection after pulling back to the
q
-prism

Lemma 3.1.1
.

Let
R=A[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}-q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\epsilon)=A\oplus\epsilon A
. Then the
W(k)
-linear ring homomorphism
\psi:A\to R
sending
q
to
q+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}
induces a ring homomorphism
A/([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}^{n})\to R/([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}^{n})
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, which will still be denoted as
\psi
by abuse of notation.

Proof.

As
A
is topologically free over
W(k)
, any
W(k)
-linear ring homomorphism from
A
to
R
is uniquely determined by its image of
q
. Next by induction we calculate that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\psi(q^{k})=q^{k}+\epsilon q^{k-1}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}=q^{k}+\epsilon[pk]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{k-1}.

For
k=1
, this holds by the definition of
\psi
. Suppose it holds up to
k
, then a direct induction shows that

\begin{split}\psi(q^{k+1})&=\psi(q^{k})\cdot\psi(q)=(q^{k}+\epsilon q^{k-1}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}})(q+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\\
&=q^{k+1}+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{k}(1+[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}})+\epsilon^{2}q^{k-1}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}^{2}[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}\\
&=q^{k+1}+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{k}(1+[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}})+\epsilon q^{k}\cdot(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\cdot(\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1})^{2}\cdot\frac{q^{kp^{\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}\\
&=q^{k+1}+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{k}(1+[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}+q^{kp^{\alpha+1}}-1)\\
&=q^{k+1}+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{k}[k+1]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}},\end{split}

hence the claim works for all positive integers.

It then follows that
\psi([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})=\psi(\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{p^{\alpha}i})=[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}(\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{p^{\alpha}i-1}[p^{\alpha}i]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}})
, hence
\psi([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\in([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})R
, which further implies that
\psi
induces a ring homomorphism
A/([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}^{n})\to R/([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}^{n})
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Remark 3.1.2
.

One can check that the composition of
\psi
with the quotient map
R\to A
sending
\epsilon
to
q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)
is an automorphism of
A
under which
q
is taken to
q^{p^{\alpha+1}+1}
. We will denote this automorphism as
\gamma_{A}
for later use. In this sense,
\psi(f)=f+\epsilon\partial_{A}(f)
for

\begin{split}\partial_{A}:A&\longrightarrow A\\
f&\longmapsto\frac{\gamma_{A}(f)-f}{q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)}.\end{split}

Note that
\partial_{A}
is only
W(k)
-linear.

Remark 3.1.3
.

If
\alpha=0
, then
\psi(q^{k})=q^{k}+\epsilon[pk]_{q}q^{k-1}
. One can compare
\psi
with the
q(p)
-derivation in
[
GSQ23
]
. Roughly speaking,
\psi=\mathrm{Id}+\epsilon\partial_{q(p)}
, here
\partial_{q(p)}
is the
absolute
q(p)
-derivation
defined in loc. cit.

Remark 3.1.4
.

We upgrade
R
to a
\delta
-ring extending that structure on
A
by requiring that
\varphi(\epsilon)=q^{p-1}\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}\epsilon
. Actually, as
(p,q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)
forms a regular sequence in
R
, one see that

\varphi(\epsilon)-\epsilon^{p}=\frac{q^{p-1}\epsilon}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}(q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1-(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)^{p})

is zero modulo
p
, hence
\varphi
is indeed a lift of the Fribenius modulo
p
.

Proposition 3.1.5
.

For
R=A[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}-q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\epsilon)=A\oplus\epsilon A
as in the previous lemma, there exists a unique
b
in
W(R)^{\times}
such that the following holds:

•

\tilde{g}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})=\tilde{f}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\cdot b
, where
\tilde{g}
is the unique
\delta
-ring map such that the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

and
\tilde{f}
is defined similarly with the bottom line in the above diagram replaced with the canonical embedding
A\hookrightarrow R
.

Proof.

Notice that the
W(k)
-linear algebra morphism
\tilde{g}
is uniquely characterized by the following two properties:

•

p_{0}(\tilde{g}(q))=q+[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}
.

•

\varphi(\tilde{g}(q))=\tilde{g}(\varphi(q))
.

Now we wish to construct
b=(b_{0},b_{1},\ldots)
such that
\tilde{g}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})=\tilde{f}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})\cdot b
. As
R
is
p
-torsion free, the ghost map is injective, hence this identity is equivalent to that

(3.1)

\displaystyle\forall n\geq 0,w_{n}(\tilde{g}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}))=w_{n}(b)\cdot w_{n}(\tilde{f}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})).

Here
w_{n}
denotes the
n
-th ghost map.

We first make the three terms showing up in
Eq.

3.1
more explicit. In the following, we denote
[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}
as
d
for simplicity. Notice that

w_{n}(\tilde{f}(d))=w_{0}(\varphi^{n}(\tilde{f}(d)))=w_{0}(\tilde{f}(\varphi^{n}(d)))=\varphi^{n}(d),

Here the second equality follows as
\tilde{f}
commutes with
\varphi
. Similarly, using the formula that
\psi(q^{k})=q^{k}+\epsilon q^{k-1}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[k]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}
obtained in
Lemma

3.1.1
, for
n\geq 1
, we have that

(3.2)

\begin{split}w_{n}(\tilde{g}((d))&=w_{0}(g(\varphi^{n}(d)))=\psi([p]_{q^{p^{n+\alpha}}})=\varphi^{n}(d)+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{n+\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}\\
&=\varphi^{n}(d)+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n}}}\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}}\cdot[p^{n-1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}\\
&=\varphi^{n}(d)(1+\epsilon[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[p^{n-1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}})\\
&=\varphi^{n}(d)(1+\epsilon[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}}).\end{split}

Here the second line holds by
Lemma

3.1.7
. Then a direct calculation shows that
w_{n}(\tilde{g}((d))=\varphi^{n}(d)(1+\epsilon[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}})
also holds for
n=0
.

Define
r_{n}=1+\epsilon[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}}
. As
R
is
\varphi^{n}(d)
-torsion free for all
n
(for example, see
[
ALB23
, Lemma 2.1.7]
),
Eq.

3.1
holds if and only if the ghost coordinates of
b
is precisely given by
(r_{0},r_{1},\cdots)
. In other words, we aim to show that
(r_{0},r_{1},\cdots)\in R^{\mathbb{N}}
is the ghost coordinate for some element
b\in W(R)
(such
b
is unique if exists as ghost map is injective). As
R
is a
\delta
-ring thanks to
Remark

3.1.4
, by invoking Dwork’s lemma (see
[
Laz06
, 4.6 on page 213]
for details), it suffices to show that for any
n\geq 0
,

p^{n+1}|\varphi(r_{n})-r_{n+1}.

Given our specific formulas for
r_{n}
, we just need to check that for any
1\leq i\leq p-1
,

\varphi(\epsilon[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}})-\epsilon[p^{n+1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{ip^{n+\alpha+1}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+2}}}

is divisible by
p^{n+1}
, but actually it vanishes:

\begin{split}&\varphi(\epsilon[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{ip^{n+\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}})-\epsilon[p^{n+1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{ip^{n+\alpha+1}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+2}}}\\
=&\epsilon q^{p-1}\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha+1}-p}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+2}}}-\epsilon\frac{q^{p^{n+\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}q^{ip^{n+\alpha+1}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+2}}}\\
=&0.\end{split}

∎

Remark 3.1.6
.

The above proof implies that
b_{0}=1+\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}q^{ip^{\alpha}-1}[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}
. In particular, the image of it after modulo
d
is
1+\epsilon\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}ip^{\alpha}q^{ip^{\alpha}-1}
, which will be used later.

The following lemma is used in the above proof:

Lemma 3.1.7
.

Keep notations as in the above lemma. For any
n\geq 1,i\geq 0
,

[ip^{n+\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}=[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n}}}\cdot[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}}\cdot[p^{n-1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}.

Proof.

This follows from the observation that

\begin{split}[ip^{n+\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}&=\frac{(q^{p^{\alpha+1}})^{ip^{n+\alpha}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}=\frac{q^{ip^{n+1+2\alpha}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}{q^{p^{n+\alpha}}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{n+\alpha}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}\\
&=[ip^{\alpha}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}}\cdot[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha+n}}}\cdot[p^{n-1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}.\end{split}

∎

Next we state a result which will be used together with
Proposition

3.1.5
to construct the desired Sen operator.

Proposition 3.1.8
.

Keep notations as in
Proposition

3.1.5
, there exists a unique
c
in
W(R)
such that

\tilde{g}(q)-\tilde{f}(q)=\tilde{f}(d)\cdot c.

Proof.

We wish to construct
c=(c_{0},c_{1},\ldots)
such that
\tilde{g}(q)-\tilde{f}(q)=\tilde{f}(d)\cdot c
. As
R
is
p
-torsion free, the ghost map is injective, hence this identity is equivalent to that

(3.3)

\displaystyle\forall n\geq 0,w_{n}(\tilde{g}(q))-w_{n}(\tilde{f}(q))=w_{n}(c)\cdot w_{n}(\tilde{f}(d)),

where
w_{n}
denotes the
n
-th ghost map.
Notice that

\begin{split}&w_{n}(\tilde{f}(q))=w_{0}(\varphi^{n}(\tilde{f}(q)))=w_{0}(\tilde{f}(\varphi^{n}(q)))=q^{p^{n}},\\
&w_{n}(\tilde{g}(q))=w_{0}(\varphi^{n}(\tilde{g}(q)))=w_{0}(\tilde{g}(\varphi^{n}(q)))=\psi(q^{p^{n}})=q^{p^{n}}+\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}},\\
&w_{n}(\tilde{f}(d))=\varphi^{n}([p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})=[p]_{q^{p^{n+\alpha}}}.\end{split}

Consequently,

\begin{split}&w_{n}(\tilde{g}(q))-w_{n}(\tilde{f}(q))=\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}}\\
=&\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}\\
=&\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+n}}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}\\
=&\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}[p]_{q^{p^{n+\alpha}}}\cdot[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}\end{split}

We define
r_{n}=\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}\cdot[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}
. As
R
is
\varphi^{n}(d)
-torsion free for all
n
,
Eq.

3.3
holds if and only if the ghost coordinates of
c
is precisely given by
(r_{0},r_{1},\cdots)
. In other words, we aim to show that
(r_{0},r_{1},\cdots)\in R^{\mathbb{N}}
is the ghost coordinate for some element
c\in W(R)
(such
c
is unique if exists as ghost map is injective). As
R
is a
\delta
-ring thanks to
Remark

3.1.4
, by invoking Dwork’s lemma (see
[
Laz06
, 4.6 on page 213]
for details), it suffices to show that for any
n\geq 0
,

p^{n+1}|\varphi(r_{n})-r_{n+1}.

But
\varphi(r_{n})-r_{n+1}
actually vanishes as

\begin{split}&\varphi(r_{n})-r_{n+1}=\varphi(\epsilon q^{p^{n}-1}\cdot[p^{n}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}})-\epsilon q^{p^{n+1}-1}\cdot[p^{n+1}]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}\\
=&\epsilon\cdot q^{p-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}q^{p^{n+1}-p}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha+1}}-1}-\epsilon q^{p^{n+1}-1}\cdot\frac{q^{p^{\alpha+n+1}}-1}{q^{p^{\alpha}}-1}\\
=&0.\end{split}

Hence we finish the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to construct the
q
-Higgs connection for quasi-coherent complexes on
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, based on the following key proposition.

Proposition 3.1.9
.

The elements
b
and
c
constructed in
Proposition

3.1.5
and
Proposition

3.1.8
together induce an isomorphism
\gamma_{b,c}
between functors
\rho:\mathrm{Spf}(R)\xrightarrow{\iota}\mathrm{Spf}(A)\rightarrow X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}

and
\rho\circ\psi:\mathrm{Spf}(R)\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathrm{Spf}(A)\rightarrow X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, i.e. we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{b,c}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}

Proof.

Given a test
(p,d)
-nilpotent
R
-algebra
T
via the structure morphism
h:R\to T
, we denote the induced morphism
W(R)\to W(T)
by
\tilde{h}
. Then
\rho\circ h(T)
corresponds to the point

(\alpha:(d)\otimes_{A,\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}}W(T)\to W(T),\eta:\Cone((d)\to A)\xrightarrow{\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}}\Cone(\alpha))

in
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}(T)
, while
(\rho\circ\psi)\circ h(T)
corresponds to the point

(\alpha^{\prime}:(d)\otimes_{A,\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{g}}W(T)\to W(T),\eta^{\prime}:\Cone((d)\to A)\xrightarrow{\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{g}}\Cone(\alpha^{\prime})).

We need to specify an isomorphism
\gamma_{b}:\alpha^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\alpha
as well as a homotopy
\gamma_{c}
between
\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}
and
\eta
which are both functorial in
T
.

Utilizing
Proposition

3.1.5
, we construct the desired isomorphism
\gamma_{b}
as follows:

\textstyle{(d)\otimes_{A,\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{g}}W(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(d)\otimes x\mapsto(d)\otimes\tilde{h}(b)x}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{W(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Id}}
\textstyle{(d)\otimes_{A,\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}}W(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{W(T)}

Here the left vertical map is
W(T)
-linear and the commutativity of the diagram follows from
Proposition

3.1.5
.

Then we draw a diagram illustrating
\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}
and
\eta
(as maps of quasi-ideals):

\textstyle{(d)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{(d)\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}}W(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{W(T)}

Here the two left vertical maps are given by
\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}:x\cdot(d)\mapsto(d)\otimes\tilde{h}(b)\tilde{h}(\tilde{g}(x))
and
\eta:x\cdot(d)\mapsto(d)\otimes\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}(x)
, the two right vertical maps are given by
\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}
and
\eta=\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}
.

To construct the desired homotopy, we need to specify a map
\gamma_{c}:A\to(d)\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\tilde{h}\circ\tilde{f}}W(T)
such that
\iota\circ\gamma_{c}=\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}-\eta
and that
\gamma_{c}\circ\iota=\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}-\eta
. Without loss of generality, we assume
T=R
. In this case,
\iota:(d)\otimes_{A,\tilde{f}}W(R)\to W(R)
is injective as
W(R)
is
d
-torsion free by the uniqueness in
Proposition

3.1.5
(otherwise if there exists a nontrivial
d
-torsion
q
,
b+q\neq b
is another objects in
W(R)
satisfying
(b+q)\cdot d=d
, a contradiction with the uniqueness of
b
). Consequently, it suffices to construct
\gamma_{c}
and check that
\iota\circ\gamma_{c}=\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}-\eta
.

Inspired by
Proposition

3.1.8
, we just define
\gamma_{c}(q)
to be
d\otimes_{\mathfrak{S},\tilde{f}}c
. For a general
s(q)\in A
, denote
k_{s}(u,v)\in W(k)[[u,v]]
to be the unique power series such that
s(u)-s(v)=(u-v)\cdot k_{s}(u,v)
. Then

(\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}-\eta)(s(u))=\tilde{g}(s(u))-\tilde{f}(s(u))=s(\tilde{g}(u))-s(\tilde{f}(u))=(\tilde{g}(u)-\tilde{f}(u))\cdot k_{s}=\tilde{f}(d)c\cdot k_{s}

for
k_{s}=k_{s}(\tilde{g}(u),\tilde{f}(u))\in W(R)
. Here the last quality follows from our construction of
c
in
Proposition

3.1.8
.

Hence if we define

\gamma_{c}(s(q))=(d)\otimes_{A,\tilde{f}}ck_{s},

Then the desired identity
\iota\circ\gamma_{c}=\gamma_{b}\circ\eta^{\prime}-\eta
follows.

Finally it is clear that
\gamma_{b}
and
\gamma_{c}
are all constructed via a base change from
W(R)
to
W(T)
, hence they are all natural in
T
. We win.
∎

When restricted to the locus where
(t)^{n}=0
inside
[\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}[[t]])/\mathbf{G}_{m}]
, we obtain the following truncated version of
Proposition

3.1.9
.

Corollary 3.1.10
.

The
\gamma_{b,c}
constructed in
Proposition

3.1.9
induces an isomorphism between functors
\rho:\mathrm{Spf}(R/d^{n})\to\mathrm{Spf}(A/d^{n})\rightarrow X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}

and
\rho\circ\psi:\mathrm{Spf}(R/d^{n})\xrightarrow{\psi}\mathrm{Spf}(A/d^{n})\rightarrow X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, i.e. we have the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(R/d^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A/d^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{b,c}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Spf}(A/d^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Now we are ready to construct a
q
-Higgs derivation on
\rho^{*}\mathscr{E}
for
\mathscr{E}\in\Qcoh(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
(resp.
\Qcoh(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
). Based on
Proposition

3.1.9
(resp.
Corollary

3.1.10
), we have an isomorphism
\gamma_{b,c}:\rho\circ\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho
. Consequently, for
\mathscr{E}\in\Qcoh(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
(resp.
\Qcoh(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
), we have an isomorphism

\gamma_{b,c}:\psi^{*}\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho^{*}\mathscr{E}\otimes_{A}R.

Unwinding the definitions, this could be identified with a
\psi
-linear morphism

(3.4)

\displaystyle\gamma_{b,c}:\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E})\rightarrow\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E})\otimes_{A}R.

Moreover, our definition of the element
b
and
c
in
Proposition

3.1.5
and
Proposition

3.1.8
implies that
\gamma_{b,c}
in
Eq.

3.4
reduces to the identity modulo
\epsilon
, hence could be written as
\mathrm{Id}+\epsilon\partial_{\mathscr{E}}
for some operator
\partial_{\mathscr{E}}:\rho^{*}\mathscr{E}\rightarrow\rho^{*}\mathscr{E}
(as
R=A[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}-q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\epsilon)=A\oplus A\epsilon
), which we will refer to as the
q
-connection
on the complex
\rho^{*}\mathscr{E}
.

Next we explain how the pullback along
\rho
induces a functor from the category of quasi-coherent complexes on
X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
to the derived category of modules over a certain non-commutative ring.

First we observe that given a pair
(M,\gamma_{M})
such that
M
is a discrete
A
-module and
\gamma_{M}:\psi^{*}M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}M\otimes_{A,\iota}R
is an isomorphism reducing to the identify after modulo
\epsilon
, then we can extract a monodromy operator on
M
satisfying a twisted Leibnitz rule.

Lemma 3.1.11
.

Given a pair
(M,\gamma_{M})
as above, we can write
\gamma_{M}
as
\mathrm{Id}+\epsilon\partial_{M}
when restricted to
M
for a operator
\partial_{M}:M\to M
, then we have that

\partial_{M}(ax)=a\partial_{M}(x)+\partial_{A}(a)x+q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\partial_{A}(a)\partial_{M}(x)=\gamma_{A}(a)\partial_{M}(x)+\partial_{A}(a)x

for
a\in A
and
x\in M
. Here
\partial_{A}:A\to A
(defined in
Remark

3.1.2
) is
W(k)
-linear and sends
q^{i}
to
[pi]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{i-1}
. In particular,

\partial_{M}(qx)=[p]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}x+q^{p^{\alpha+1}+1}\partial_{M}(x).

Proof.

The characterization of
\partial
on
A
follows as
\gamma
is
\psi
-linear and
\psi(q^{i})=q^{i}+\epsilon[pi]_{q^{p^{\alpha}}}q^{i-1}
. For a general pair
(M,\gamma_{M})
,

\begin{split}&\gamma_{M}(ax)=\psi(a)\gamma_{M}(x)=(a+\epsilon\partial(a))(x+\epsilon\partial_{M}(x))\\
=&ax+\epsilon(a\partial_{M}(x)+\partial(a)x)+\epsilon^{2}\partial(a)\partial_{M}(x)\\
=&ax+\epsilon(a\partial_{M}(x)+\partial(a)x)+\epsilon q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\partial(a)\partial_{M}(x)\\
=&ax+\epsilon(a\partial_{M}(x)+\partial(a)x+q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\partial(a)\partial_{M}(x)).\end{split}

This implies the desired result.
∎

Remark 3.1.12
.

\partial_{M}
preserves the
d
-adic filtration, i.e.
\partial(dM)\subseteq dM
as
\partial(d)\in dA
.

Remark 3.1.13
.

More generally, let
\mathscr{E}
and
\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
be quasi-coherent complexes on
X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
, and let
\mathscr{E}\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\prime}
denote their derived tensor product. Then the same argument shows that the
q
-connection
\partial_{\mathscr{E}\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\prime}}
on
\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E}\otimes\mathscr{E}^{\prime})\cong\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E})\otimes\rho^{*}(\mathscr{E}^{\prime})
can be identified with
\partial_{\mathscr{E}}\otimes\mathrm{Id}_{\mathscr{E}^{\prime}}+\mathrm{Id}_{\mathscr{E}}\otimes\partial_{\mathscr{E}^{\prime}}+q(q^{p^{\alpha}}-1)\partial_{\mathscr{E}}\otimes\partial_{\mathscr{E}^{\prime}}
.

Motivated by
Lemma

3.1.11
, the following skew polynomial enters the picture.

Definition 3.1.14
.

Let
\gamma_{A}:A\to A
be the ring automorphism defined in
Remark

3.1.2
, then
\partial_{A}:A\to A
introduced in
Remark

3.1.2
is a
\gamma_{A}
-derivation of
A
, i.e.
\partial_{A}(x_{1}x_{2})=\gamma_{A}(x_{1})\partial_{A}(x_{2})+\partial_{A}(x_{1})x_{2}
. We define the
Ore extension

A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
to be the noncommutative ring obtained by giving the ring of polynomials
A[\partial]
a new multiplication law, subject to the identity

\partial r=\gamma_{A}(r)\partial+\partial_{A}(r),~~~\forall r\in A.

Remark 3.1.15
.

For any
n\geq 1
, as
\gamma_{A}
(resp.
\partial_{A}
) induces a ring automorphism
A/d^{n}\to A/d^{n}
(resp. a
\gamma_{A}
-derivation of
A/d^{n}
), we can similarly define
A/d^{n}[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
12
12

12

We want to point out that by
Remark

3.1.12
,
d^{n}
is a two sided ideal inside
A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
and
A/d^{n}[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
defined in this way is actually isomorphic to
A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]/d^{n}
.
. Note that one
n=1
,
A/d[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
is just the usual (commutative) polynomial ring
A/d[\partial]
as
\gamma_{A}
reduces to the identity after modulo
d
and
\partial_{A}
vanishes on
A/d
.

Remark 3.1.16
.

One can check that
A
is isomorphic to the quotient of
A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
by the left ideal generated by
\partial
. More precisely, there is a canonical resolution for
A
by finite free
A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]
-modules:

[A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f\mapsto f\cdot\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}]]\simeq A.

Consequently, for
M\in\mathcal{D}(A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}])
,

\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}])}(A,M)\simeq[M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}M].

Such an observation will be used to calculate the cohomology later.

For the purpose of upgrading the pullback along
\rho
to a functor from
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
to
\mathcal{D}(A[\partial;\gamma_{A},\partial_{A}])
, the last missing piece is to show that
\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
is equivalent to the derived category of its heart. We first construct a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
following
[
GL23
, Section 2.1]
, where they study
t
-structures on perfect complexes of prismatic crystals.

Construction 3.1.17
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
and
(A,I)
be the
q
-prism as usual. We denote the Cech nerve of this cover by
\mathrm{Spf}(A^{\bullet})
. By
Proposition

2.0.4
,
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\lim_{(A,I)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}(A/I^{n})
, which is then equivalent to the cosimplicial limit of the derived category of quasi-coherent complexes on
\mathrm{Spf}(A^{\bullet}/I^{n})
as the latter forms a cofinal system. we define a
t
-sturcture on
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
by requiring that
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
lives in
\leq 0
part (resp.
\geq 0
part) if the underlying complexes on
\mathrm{Spf}(A^{\bullet})
is concentrated on degree
\leq 0
(resp.
\geq 0
). We call this the
standard
t
-structure
, which deserves the name by the following proposition.

Proposition 3.1.18
.

Construction
3.1.17
defines a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
. Moreover, given any
(B,J)\in X_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}
such that
\mathrm{Spf}(B/J)\to X
is
p
-adically flat, the pullback functor functor
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})\to\mathcal{D}(B/J^{n})
is
t
-exact.

Proof.

The proof of
[
GL23
, Lemma 2.9, Proposition 2.11]
works verbatim here. Note that the
q
-prism
(A,I)
belongs to the category they denote as “Breuil-Kisin prisms" in
[
GL23
, Definition 2.4]
.
∎

Remark 3.1.19
.

Proposition

3.1.18
tells us being in the heart of the
t
-structure with respect to our chosen family
\mathrm{Spf}(A^{\bullet})
guarantees that its evaluation at any other prism
(B,J)\in X{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}
covering the final object is concentrated on degree
0
as well, hence is independent of our chosen cover
\mathrm{Spf}(A^{\bullet})
(we can run Construction
3.1.17
and
Proposition

3.1.18
to any chosen cover).

From now on we denote the heart of
\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
with respect to the standard
t
structure as
\mathcal{C}_{n}
. Note that for
m<n
, there is a natural functor
i_{m,n}:\mathcal{C}_{m}\to\mathcal{C}_{n}
which admits a left adjoint functor given by tensoring with the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X_{n-1}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}
. It induces a derived functor
Li_{m,n}^{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\to\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{m})
, which is compatible with the natural functors
r_{n}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\to\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
as
r_{n}
preserves projective objects.

Proposition 3.1.20
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the the natural functor
r_{n}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})\to\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})
is an equivalence.

Proof.

It suffices to show full faithfulness as the essential surjectivity will then follow immediately from
Proposition

3.3.3
. For this purpose, we first explain how to reduce to the Hodge-Tate case by doing induction on
n
. Suppose we have proved the statement up to
n-1
(
n\geq 2
). Then we wish to show that for any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})
,

(3.5)

\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(X_{n}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})}(r_{n}(\mathcal{F}),r_{n}(\mathcal{G})).

For this purpose, first we notice that there is a fiber sequence (see
[
Liu24
, Proposition 3.15]
for details)

(3.6)

\mathcal{G}\otimes((i_{n-1,n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n-1}}\{1\})\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\otimes((i_{1,n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{WCart}^{\operatorname{HT}}}).

Then we observe that

\begin{split}&\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\otimes((i_{n-1,n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n-1}}\{1\}))=\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}(\mathcal{F},(i_{n-1,n})_{*}L(i_{n-1,n})^{*}\mathcal{G}\{1\})\\
=&\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n-1})}(Li_{n-1,n}^{*}\mathcal{F},Li_{n-1,n}^{*}\mathcal{G}\{1\})=\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(X^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}_{n-1})}(r_{n-1}(Li_{n-1,n}^{*}\mathcal{F}),r_{n-1}(Li_{n-1,n}^{*}\mathcal{G}\{1\})),\end{split}

where the first identity is due to the projection formula, the second equation follows from adjunction and the last identity holds by induction. A similar calculation for
\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}\otimes((i_{1,n})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{WCart}^{\operatorname{HT}}}))
implies the desired
Eq.

3.5
.

For
n=\infty
, we observe that any
\mathcal{G}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{\infty})
is complete with respect to the Hodge-Tate ideal sheaf
\mathcal{I}
, hence
\mathcal{G}=\varprojlim\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{G}
, which implies that for any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{\infty})
,

\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{\infty})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})=\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{\infty})}(\mathcal{F},\varprojlim\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{G})=\varprojlim\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{C}_{n})}(\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{I}^{n}\mathcal{F}).

One then similarly calculates
\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{D}(X_{\infty}^{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}})}(r_{\infty}(\mathcal{F}),r_{\infty}(\mathcal{G}))
and the desired results follows from induction.

To prove the statement for
X^{\operatorname{HT}}
, a similar reduction process as above implies that it suffices to work with
X^{\operatorname{HT}}\otimes k
instead. But as
X^{\operatorname{HT}}
is the classifying stack of
G_{\pi}
over
X
by
[
BL22b
, Proposition 9.5]
(see
[
BL22b
, Example 9.6]
for an explicit description of
G_{\pi}
), hence
X^{\operatorname{HT}}\otimes k=BG_{\pi,k}
. Then the proof of
[
AHB22
, Theorem 2.5]
